Problem This Solves\\n\\nOrchestrators (Kubernetes, Fly, Render, container platforms) have no probe that answers \\\"has the Gateway finished starting?\\\" without also folding in downstream channel health. `/readyz` flips to 503 when any channel account hard-fails (`blocked`, `terminal-disconnect`, post-grace `not-running` — `src/gateway/server/readiness.ts`), so one broken Telegram account takes an otherwise healthy Gateway and Control UI out of traffic rotation or blocks a rollout. There is also no HTTP surface exposing the running version, which makes \\\"which build is this deployment actually running?\\\" needlessly hard to answer for cloud operators.\\n\\nThe bundled deployment templates compound this:\\n\\n- `fly.toml` declared no health checks at all.\\n- `render.yaml` used `/health` (pure liveness) as Render's traffic-admission signal.\\n- The Kubernetes manifest combined the mutable `:slim` tag with `imagePullPolicy: IfNotPresent` (upgrades silently never happen) and an init container that unconditionally overwrote the persisted `openclaw.json` and workspace `AGENTS.md` on every pod start, erasing product-made edits.\\n- `docs/install/fly.md` still claimed the image `HEALTHCHECK` is pinned to port 18789; stale since `bc4221a07e9` (#116639) made `docker-healthcheck` resolve the active Gateway lock port.\\n\\n## Why This Change Was Made\\n\\nFirst step of the cloud-container deployment track: give every host one correct, additive probe contract before building anything platform-specific. `/readyz` semantics are a shipped public contract (existing manifests probe it), so this adds a probe rather than changing one.\\n\\n- New `/startup` + `/startupz` routes: `200 {\\\"ok\\\":true,\\\"status\\\":\\\"started\\\"}` once startup work is complete and the Gateway is not draining; `503` with `status:\\\"starting\\\"` (plus `pendingReason` for privileged callers) during startup; `503` with `status:\\\"draining\\\"` during drain. Channel health is never consulted.\\n- The startup policy is one factory (`createStartupChecker`); the readiness checker now composes it instead of duplicating the startup/draining branches.\\n- Version (`resolveRuntimeServiceVersion`) and `uptimeMs` are included only for local-direct or authenticated callers, reusing the exact detail-gating already applied to `/readyz` subsystem names (now a shared helper). Remote unauthenticated probes get `{ok,status}` only — no build fingerprinting surface.\\n- Templates now use `/startupz` for startup/traffic admission, keep `/healthz` for liveness, and leave `/readyz` for channel-aware monitoring. K8s image pinned to the immutable `2026.7.1-2-slim` tag; init container seeds config only when missing.\\n\\n## User Impact\\n\\n- A failed channel account no longer evicts a healthy Gateway/Control UI from Service endpoints or fails platform health checks on the bundled templates.\\n- Operators and supervisors can read the running Gateway version over HTTP with their existing token (or locally) — no WebSocket handshake needed.\\n- Kubernetes deployments stop silently ignoring upgrades and stop losing config edits on pod restart.\\n- No behavior change to `/healthz`, `/readyz`, or any existing response shape. No new config or env surface.\\n\\n## Evidence\\n\\n- Regression proof: the new assertions fail on pre-change code (`node scripts/run-vitest.mjs src/gateway/server-http.probe.test.ts src/gateway/server.plugin-http-auth.test.ts` → 6 failed pre-fix, all for the missing routes/factory).\\n- Post-fix: `node scripts/run-vitest.mjs src/gateway/server-http.probe.test.ts src/gateway/server.plugin-http-auth.test.ts src/gateway/server/readiness.test.ts` green (output in PR checks / see below).\\n- New coverage: startup lifecycle transitions (starting → started → draining), hard channel failure flips `/readyz` but not `/startupz`, detail gating (remote unauthenticated gets minimal body; authenticated gets version/uptime/pendingReason), GET/HEAD parity with Content-Length, 404 on suffixed probe paths.\\n- K8s image tag verified against Docker Hub's exact-tag API (active amd64/arm64 manifest) and the `<release-tag>-slim` publication rule in `.github/workflows/docker-release.yml`.\\n- Docs updated: `docs/gateway/health.md` (probe table + semantics), `docs/install/{docker,fly,kubernetes,render}` including the corrected Fly healthcheck-port explanation.\\n\", \"reactions\": { \"url\": \"https://api.github.com/repos/openclaw/openclaw/issues/122477/reactions\", \"total_count\": 1, \"+1\": 0, \"-1\": 0, \"laugh\": 0, \"hooray\": 0, Summary\\n\\n### High Level TLDR\\n\\nA quota failure on one auth profile could pause the shared command lane for 30 minutes, blocking unrelated sessions and providers before they reached a model. A user-selected auth profile also collapsed same-provider auth routing to one candidate, so a second configured account was never tried.\\n\\nThis removes provider-failure control from command-lane capacity and makes a user auth pin a durable first preference instead of a strict singleton. Only the failed profile enters cooldown; eligible same-provider profiles remain available. Explicit provider/model selection stays strict.\\n\\n- keep quota/manual/circuit suspension markers for diagnostics and recovery, without changing lane concurrency\\n- try a valid user-pinned profile first, then ordered same-provider profiles\\n- skip only a cooled/disabled profile and return to the persisted pin after recovery\\n- preserve strict provider bindings and explicit provider/model selection\\n- include compatible external-CLI credential sources when discovering sibling profiles\\n- keep shutdown fencing for late suspension-marker writes\\n\\n## What Problem This Solves\\n\\nA failure on one provider credential must not pause unrelated work or suppress other authorized credentials for that provider. Before this change, one terminal quota failure could set a shared command lane to zero for 30 minutes, while a user-sourced auth override prevented the runner from trying a configured sibling profile.\\n\\n## Evidence\\n\\n- Redacted live evidence showed one OpenAI quota failure setting the shared `main` lane to zero while an unrelated provider session waited before model invocation.\\n- Exact-head queue integration proves a suspension marker leaves unrelated `main`-lane work runnable.\\n- Exact-head runner E2E proves a user-pinned profile rotates to a same-provider backup in both OpenClaw and Codex harness paths, then surfaces failure only after all eligible profiles fail.\\n- Exact-head results: 314 focused tests and 47 runner E2Es passed; benchmark results are recorded below.\\n\\n## Root Cause\\n\\n### Before behavior\\n\\n1. `suspendSession` accepted a command-lane id, called `setCommandLaneConcurrency(lane, 0)`, and scheduled a default 30-minute resume timer.\\n2. Ordinary agent turns from different sessions and providers share the `main` lane. One terminal provider failure therefore blocked unrelated work at queue admission, before provider/model routing.\\n3. Runtime auth planning encoded a user-selected profile as required `user-lock` ownership. That produced one physical auth attempt and bypassed normal same-provider cooldown admission, even when another authorized profile existed.\\n4. External-CLI discovery also narrowed a user pin to one credential source before the planner could see ordered siblings.\\n\\nThe earlier repair in #93789 correctly deferred lane suspension while model fallback candidates remained, but terminal/direct failures could still lock a shared lane. The remaining invariant violation was architectural: provider/profile availability was controlling a process-wide scheduling primitive.\\n\\nThe Codex dependency contract confirms that one app-server client has one active `auth_manager`/account and surfaces usage-limit reset metadata. Cross-account selection is therefore OpenClaw's responsibility at its prepared profile/client boundary, not something one Codex client performs internally:\\n\\n- https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/2f108f9fd970ed73df7e984d3a661acc02f33abc/codex-rs/app-server/src/request_processors/account_processor.rs#L818-L839\\n- https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/2f108f9fd970ed73df7e984d3a661acc02f33abc/codex-rs/protocol/src/error.rs#L449-L550\\n\\n### RCA evidence\\n\\nA redacted production incident reproduced the complete ordering:\\n\\n- one OpenAI subscription profile returned a usage-limit error\\n- the exact profile received cooldown/reset metadata\\n- the terminal suspension path set the shared `main` lane to concurrency zero\\n- a separate session using an unrelated provider remained queued before any model call\\n- a second configured OpenAI profile was not attempted because the session auth override was user-sourced\\n\\nThe deployed base, active overlay patches, and persisted runtime state were inspected separately. No overlay touched the causal lane-suspension/profile-planning ownership paths.\\n\\n## Real behavior proof\\n\\nClosest feasible environment: exact-head runner E2E with the real auth planner/controller and mocked provider transport, plus the real process command queue.\\n\\n```text\\n$ node scripts/run-vitest.mjs \\\\\\n src/agents/model-fallback.run-embedded.e2e.test.ts \\\\\\n src/agents/embedded-agent-runner.run-embedded-agent.auth-profile-rotation.e2e.test.ts\\n\\nOpenClaw 2026.8.1 (82342d0)\\nTest Files 2 passed (2)\\nTests 47 passed (47)\\n```\\n\\nThe E2E matrix proves:\\n\\n- a rate-limited user pin rotates to the next same-provider profile\\n- the failed pin receives cooldown while the sibling succeeds\\n- a cooled pin is skipped on the next turn without replacing the persisted preference\\n- Codex-harness retry changes from the user-pinned OpenAI profile to the backup OpenAI profile and attributes the backup as automatic\\n- only after all same-provider profiles fail does the terminal failure surface\\n\\nThe queue integration proves an OpenAI suspension marker does not block unrelated `main`-lane work.\\n\\nPerformance benchmark: Node 25.9.0, same host/workload, 3 warmups plus 30 measured samples, using a 10 ms suspension TTL to measure unrelated `main`-lane admission.\\n\\n```text\\nbaseline ce53f7e82e2: median 10.181346 ms, p95 10.536215 ms\\nfinal 82342d00b00: median 0.012239 ms, p95 0.044799 ms\\n```\\n\\nThis measures removal of the artificial queue wait; it is not a provider latency benchmark.\\n\\n## What was not tested\\n\\n- The patched branch was not deployed to the production Gateway, and no real account was deliberately driven into quota exhaustion.\\n- Blacksmith Testbox validation was unavailable because its authentication had expired; With both active in the same focused empty text field, however, the first digit selects a model instead of behaving like search input, while the UI still looks like an editable field.\\n\\nThe model picker also renders those numbers as bordered keycaps, unlike the flat, muted shortcut labels already used by the sidebar session context menu.\\n\\n## Why This Change Was Made\\n\\nIn this PR, the picker opens with its trigger retaining focus. In that state, flat numeric hints are visible and `1`–`9` select the row carrying the matching hint. Focusing `Search models` fades the hints out and lets digits enter the field normally. Returning focus to the picker restores both hints and shortcuts, including after filtering.\\n\\nOpening unfocused is intentional: retaining #120495's autofocus under the new focus gate would make every shortcut hidden and inactive on open. Search remains one Tab or click away, preserving the original type-and-Enter path while giving the two input modes a clear focus boundary.\\n\\nThe hint treatment reuses the existing `.session-menu__shortcut` declarations. Opacity changes only the hint itself; the fixed action rail remains in layout throughout the transition.\\n\\n## User Impact\\n\\n- Open picker: numeric hints are visible, flat, and active.\\n- Search Problem This Solves\\n\\nThe Gateway's cooperative host-suspension handshake (`gateway.suspend.prepare|status|resume`, protocol 2026.7) had two operator dead-ends, verified live on an isolated dev A prepared Gateway rejected every new WebSocket handshake at the HTTP upgrade, so a fresh CLI or controller process could never call `gateway.suspend.resume`. The only escapes were the opt-in admin-http-rpc plugin or waiting out the two-minute lease with the Gateway refusing all work.\\n2. The upgrade rejection (HTTP 503 with an explanatory body) was never surfaced by the gateway client — callers saw an opaque `gateway closed (1006 abnormal closure (no close frame))` with no hint the Gateway was suspended. The structured connect-refusal error in the message handler was unreachable for new sockets because the upgrade gate destroyed them first.\\n\\nThere was also no CLI surface at all for the handshake.\\n\\n## Why This Change Was Made\\n\\n- **Prepared Gateways now accept authenticated WebSocket connects** ([server-http.ts](../blob/HEAD/src/gateway/server-http.ts), [message-handler.ts](../blob/HEAD/src/gateway/server/ws-connection/message-handler.ts)). The existing method gate still fences everything except `gateway.suspend.*` with retryable `UNAVAILABLE` errors, preserving the refuse-only work fence: suspension fences *work*, not control-plane visibility. Restart drain, the transient `preparing` phase, Worker ingress, and desktop-observe streams remain fully closed. This deliberately revises the shipped \\\"prepared Gateway rejects new WebSocket handshakes\\\" contract — that behavior is exactly what made resume unreachable; docs updated in the same change.\\n- **The gateway client surfaces non-101 upgrade responses** ([gateway-client/client.ts](../blob/HEAD/packages/gateway-client/src/client.ts)): bounded body read (2 KiB / 1 s), typed retryable `GatewayClientRequestError` (`websocket-upgrade-rejected`, HTTP status) carried through close/retry state so CLI callers see the real reason during restart drain.\\n- **New CLI commands** ([suspend-cli.ts](../blob/HEAD/src/cli/gateway-cli/suspend-cli.ts)): `openclaw gateway suspend` (stable request id, `--wait <seconds>` bounded polling driven by server `retryAfterMs`, blocker listing, `--json`) and `openclaw gateway resume <suspensionId>` (idempotent; expired lease reported as a clean no-op).\\n\\nProduction LOC delta is positive (~+285 excluding tests/docs/generated) and buys the new CLI capability plus the client error surface; the core gate change itself is net ~+14. The additive `connectError` field on `GatewayClientCloseInfo` regenerated the plugin-sdk API baseline.\\n\\n## User Impact\\n\\nOperators and hosting controllers can suspend and resume the Gateway end to end from the CLI: prepare before a VM snapshot/process freeze, resume from a *fresh* process after thaw. Failures name themselves: a suspended Gateway now answers \\\"`health` unavailable during gateway suspension (reason gateway-suspending, phase prepared, retryable)\\\" instead of an unexplained 1006 close. Lost-id worst case remains the 2-minute self-healing lease expiry.\\n\\n## Evidence\\n\\nLive end-to-end on an isolated dev gateway (dedicated `OPENCLAW_STATE_DIR`, port 19877, dist build):\\n\\n- `openclaw gateway suspend` → ready with suspension id; real `SIGSTOP`/`SIGCONT` freeze; `openclaw gateway resume <id>` from a fresh process → \\\"Gateway resumed.\\\"; health green over both WS and admin HTTP.\\n- While prepared: fresh-WS `gateway call health` refused with structured retryable `UNAVAILABLE` (reason `gateway-suspending`, phase `prepared`).\\n- 150 s freeze past the 120 s TTL → lease self-expires on thaw, admission reopens, late resume returns `resumed:false`, health green.\\n- Conflict (second prepare, different request id), idempotent renew (same request id), wrong-id resume (`INVALID_REQUEST`), no-suspension resume (clean no-op) all verified live.\\n\\nTests/gates (run locally; the delegated `check:changed` Testbox lane fails on its own merge-base resolution before running lanes — env-var ratchet proven locally, 502/502):\\n\\n- Focused vitest: server-methods.suspension-admission 6/6, plugins-http.suspension-admission 8/8, gateway-suspend-coordinator 13/13, message-handler.suspension-admission 5/5, server.preauth-hardening 10/10 (prepared-accept, preparing-reject, restart-reject, worker-reject), client.handshake 4/4, suspend-cli 7/7, register.option-collisions 17/17, gateway/call 133/133.\\n- `pnpm tsgo:core`, `tsgo:core:test`, oxlint on changed files, `format:check`, `check:import-cycles`, `plugin-sdk:api:check` + `surface:check`, and the remaining changed-lane aux checks all green.\\n- `pnpm build` clean (no `[INEFFECTIVE_DYNAMIC_IMPORT]`).\\n- Codex autoreview (Sol, high): clean, no actionable findings.\\n\\nFollow-up commits on this branch: the `connectError` addition pushed `protocol-client.ts` over the 700-line max-lines gate, so the adapter-facing contract types moved to a new `protocol-client-contract.ts` (no suppression; 220/220 gateway-client tests green, SDK baselines regenerated). This PR also repairs red `main`: `src/agents/mcp-oauth-refresh-issuer.test.ts` fails `check-docs` oxfmt on current main (drift from #112032's file); fixed here per red-main landing policy.\\n\", \"https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/122477.diff\", \"patch_url\": \"https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/122477.patch\", \"merged_at\": \"2026-08-12T06:36:35Z\" }, \"body\": \"## What Problem This Solves\\n\\nOrchestrators (Kubernetes, Fly, Render, container platforms) have no probe that answers \\\"has the Gateway finished starting?\\\" without also folding in downstream channel health. `/readyz` flips to 503 when any channel account hard-fails (`blocked`, `terminal-disconnect`, post-grace `not-running` — `src/gateway/server/readiness.ts`), so one broken Telegram account takes an otherwise healthy Gateway and Control UI out of traffic rotation or blocks a rollout. There is also no HTTP surface exposing the running version, which makes \\\"which build is this deployment actually running?\\\" needlessly hard to answer for cloud operators.\\n\\nThe bundled deployment templates compound this:\\n\\n- `fly.toml` declared no health checks at all.\\n- `render.yaml` used `/health` (pure liveness) as Render's traffic-admission signal.\\n- The Kubernetes manifest combined the mutable `:slim` tag with `imagePullPolicy: IfNotPresent` (upgrades silently never happen) and an init container that unconditionally overwrote the persisted `openclaw.json` and workspace `AGENTS.md` on every pod start, erasing product-made edits.\\n- `docs/install/fly.md` still claimed the image `HEALTHCHECK` is pinned to port 18789; stale since `bc4221a07e9` (#116639) made `docker-healthcheck` resolve the active Gateway lock port.\\n\\n## Why This Change Was Made\\n\\nFirst step of the cloud-container deployment track: give every host one correct, additive probe contract before building anything platform-specific. `/readyz` semantics are a shipped public contract (existing manifests probe it), so this adds a probe rather than changing one.\\n\\n- New `/startup` + `/startupz` routes: `200 {\\\"ok\\\":true,\\\"status\\\":\\\"started\\\"}` once startup work is complete and the Gateway is not draining; `503` with `status:\\\"starting\\\"` (plus `pendingReason` for privileged callers) during startup; `503` with `status:\\\"draining\\\"` during drain. Channel health is never consulted.\\n- The startup policy is one factory (`createStartupChecker`); the readiness checker now composes it instead of duplicating the startup/draining branches.\\n- Version (`resolveRuntimeServiceVersion`) and `uptimeMs` are included only for local-direct or authenticated callers, reusing the exact detail-gating already applied to `/readyz` subsystem names (now a shared helper). Remote unauthenticated probes get `{ok,status}` only — no build fingerprinting surface.\\n- Templates now use `/startupz` for startup/traffic admission, keep `/healthz` for liveness, and leave `/readyz` for channel-aware monitoring. K8s image pinned to the immutable `2026.7.1-2-slim` tag; init container seeds config only when missing.\\n\\n## User Impact\\n\\n- A failed channel account no longer evicts a healthy Gateway/Control UI from Service endpoints or fails platform health checks on the bundled templates.\\n- Operators and supervisors can read the running Gateway version over HTTP with their existing token (or locally) — no WebSocket handshake needed.\\n- Kubernetes deployments stop silently ignoring upgrades and stop losing config edits on pod restart.\\n- No behavior change to `/healthz`, `/readyz`, or any existing response shape. No new config or env surface.\\n\\n## Evidence\\n\\n- Regression proof: the new assertions fail on pre-change code (`node scripts/run-vitest.mjs src/gateway/server-http.probe.test.ts src/gateway/server.plugin-http-auth.test.ts` → 6 failed pre-fix, all for the missing routes/factory).\\n- Post-fix: `node scripts/run-vitest.mjs src/gateway/server-http.probe.test.ts src/gateway/server.plugin-http-auth.test.ts src/gateway/server/readiness.test.ts` green (output in PR checks / see below).\\n- New coverage: startup lifecycle transitions (starting → started → draining), hard channel failure flips `/readyz` but not `/startupz`, detail gating (remote unauthenticated gets minimal body; authenticated gets version/uptime/pendingReason), GET/HEAD parity with Content-Length, 404 on suffixed probe paths.\\n- K8s image tag verified against Docker Hub's exact-tag API (active amd64/arm64 manifest) and the `<release-tag>-slim` publication rule in `.github/workflows/docker-release.yml`.\\n- Docs updated: `docs/gateway/health.md` (probe table + semantics), `docs/install/{docker,fly,kubernetes,render}` including the corrected Fly healthcheck-port explanation.\\n\", \"reactions\": { \"url\": \"https://api.github.com/repos/openclaw/openclaw/issues/122477/reactions\", \"total_count\": 1, \"+1\": 0, \"-1\": 0, \"laugh\": 0, \"hooray\": 0, Summary\\n\\n### High Level TLDR\\n\\nA quota failure on one auth profile could pause the shared command lane for 30 minutes, blocking unrelated sessions and providers before they reached a model. A user-selected auth profile also collapsed same-provider auth routing to one candidate, so a second configured account was never tried.\\n\\nThis removes provider-failure control from command-lane capacity and makes a user auth pin a durable first preference instead of a strict singleton. Only the failed profile enters cooldown; eligible same-provider profiles remain available. Explicit provider/model selection stays strict.\\n\\n- keep quota/manual/circuit suspension markers for diagnostics and recovery, without changing lane concurrency\\n- try a valid user-pinned profile first, then ordered same-provider profiles\\n- skip only a cooled/disabled profile and return to the persisted pin after recovery\\n- preserve strict provider bindings and explicit provider/model selection\\n- include compatible external-CLI credential sources when discovering sibling profiles\\n- keep shutdown fencing for late suspension-marker writes\\n\\n## What Problem This Solves\\n\\nA failure on one provider credential must not pause unrelated work or suppress other authorized credentials for that provider. Before this change, one terminal quota failure could set a shared command lane to zero for 30 minutes, while a user-sourced auth override prevented the runner from trying a configured sibling profile.\\n\\n## Evidence\\n\\n- Redacted live evidence showed one OpenAI quota failure setting the shared `main` lane to zero while an unrelated provider session waited before model invocation.\\n- Exact-head queue integration proves a suspension marker leaves unrelated `main`-lane work runnable.\\n- Exact-head runner E2E proves a user-pinned profile rotates to a same-provider backup in both OpenClaw and Codex harness paths, then surfaces failure only after all eligible profiles fail.\\n- Exact-head results: 314 focused tests and 47 runner E2Es passed; benchmark results are recorded below.\\n\\n## Root Cause\\n\\n### Before behavior\\n\\n1. `suspendSession` accepted a command-lane id, called `setCommandLaneConcurrency(lane, 0)`, and scheduled a default 30-minute resume timer.\\n2. Ordinary agent turns from different sessions and providers share the `main` lane. One terminal provider failure therefore blocked unrelated work at queue admission, before provider/model routing.\\n3. Runtime auth planning encoded a user-selected profile as required `user-lock` ownership. That produced one physical auth attempt and bypassed normal same-provider cooldown admission, even when another authorized profile existed.\\n4. External-CLI discovery also narrowed a user pin to one credential source before the planner could see ordered siblings.\\n\\nThe earlier repair in #93789 correctly deferred lane suspension while model fallback candidates remained, but terminal/direct failures could still lock a shared lane. The remaining invariant violation was architectural: provider/profile availability was controlling a process-wide scheduling primitive.\\n\\nThe Codex dependency contract confirms that one app-server client has one active `auth_manager`/account and surfaces usage-limit reset metadata. Cross-account selection is therefore OpenClaw's responsibility at its prepared profile/client boundary, not something one Codex client performs internally:\\n\\n- https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/2f108f9fd970ed73df7e984d3a661acc02f33abc/codex-rs/app-server/src/request_processors/account_processor.rs#L818-L839\\n- https://github.com/openai/codex/blob/2f108f9fd970ed73df7e984d3a661acc02f33abc/codex-rs/protocol/src/error.rs#L449-L550\\n\\n### RCA evidence\\n\\nA redacted production incident reproduced the complete ordering:\\n\\n- one OpenAI subscription profile returned a usage-limit error\\n- the exact profile received cooldown/reset metadata\\n- the terminal suspension path set the shared `main` lane to concurrency zero\\n- a separate session using an unrelated provider remained queued before any model call\\n- a second configured OpenAI profile was not attempted because the session auth override was user-sourced\\n\\nThe deployed base, active overlay patches, and persisted runtime state were inspected separately. No overlay touched the causal lane-suspension/profile-planning ownership paths.\\n\\n## Real behavior proof\\n\\nClosest feasible environment: exact-head runner E2E with the real auth planner/controller and mocked provider transport, plus the real process command queue.\\n\\n```text\\n$ node scripts/run-vitest.mjs \\\\\\n src/agents/model-fallback.run-embedded.e2e.test.ts \\\\\\n src/agents/embedded-agent-runner.run-embedded-agent.auth-profile-rotation.e2e.test.ts\\n\\nOpenClaw 2026.8.1 (82342d0)\\nTest Files 2 passed (2)\\nTests 47 passed (47)\\n```\\n\\nThe E2E matrix proves:\\n\\n- a rate-limited user pin rotates to the next same-provider profile\\n- the failed pin receives cooldown while the sibling succeeds\\n- a cooled pin is skipped on the next turn without replacing the persisted preference\\n- Codex-harness retry changes from the user-pinned OpenAI profile to the backup OpenAI profile and attributes the backup as automatic\\n- only after all same-provider profiles fail does the terminal failure surface\\n\\nThe queue integration proves an OpenAI suspension marker does not block unrelated `main`-lane work.\\n\\nPerformance benchmark: Node 25.9.0, same host/workload, 3 warmups plus 30 measured samples, using a 10 ms suspension TTL to measure unrelated `main`-lane admission.\\n\\n```text\\nbaseline ce53f7e82e2: median 10.181346 ms, p95 10.536215 ms\\nfinal 82342d00b00: median 0.012239 ms, p95 0.044799 ms\\n```\\n\\nThis measures removal of the artificial queue wait; it is not a provider latency benchmark.\\n\\n## What was not tested\\n\\n- The patched branch was not deployed to the production Gateway, and no real account was deliberately driven into quota exhaustion.\\n- Blacksmith Testbox validation was unavailable because its authentication had expired; AWS Crabbox was unavailable because the broker was not logged in.\\n- A full changed-file gate could not be cleanly reproduced in the linked worktree because its borrowed dependency tree carried unrelated terminal-core and generated Plugin SDK baseline failures. \"https://api.github.com/repos/openclaw/openclaw/pulls/122100\", \"html_url\": \"https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/122100\", \"diff_url\": \"https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/122100.diff\", \"patch_url\": \"https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/pull/122100.patch\", \"merged_at\": \"2026-08-12T05:12:54Z\" }, \"body\": \"## What Problem This Solves\\n\\nThe Gateway's cooperative host-suspension handshake (`gateway.suspend.prepare|status|resume`, protocol 2026.7) had two operator dead-ends, verified live on an isolated dev gateway:\\n\\n1. A prepared Gateway rejected every new WebSocket handshake at the HTTP upgrade, so a fresh CLI or controller process could never call `gateway.suspend.resume`. The only escapes were the opt-in admin-http-rpc plugin or waiting out the two-minute lease with the Gateway refusing all work.\\n2. The upgrade rejection (HTTP 503 with an explanatory body) was never surfaced by the gateway client — callers saw an opaque `gateway closed (1006 abnormal closure (no close frame))` with no hint the Gateway was suspended. The structured connect-refusal error in the message handler was unreachable for new sockets because the upgrade gate destroyed them first.\\n\\nThere was also no CLI surface at all for the handshake.\\n\\n## Why This Change Was Made\\n\\n- **Prepared Gateways now accept authenticated WebSocket connects** ([server-http.ts](../blob/HEAD/src/gateway/server-http.ts), [message-handler.ts](../blob/HEAD/src/gateway/server/ws-connection/message-handler.ts)). The existing method gate still fences everything except `gateway.suspend.*` with retryable `UNAVAILABLE` errors, preserving the refuse-only work fence: suspension fences *work*, not control-plane visibility. Restart drain, the transient `preparing` phase, Worker ingress, and desktop-observe streams remain fully closed. This deliberately revises the shipped \\\"prepared Gateway rejects new WebSocket handshakes\\\" contract — that behavior is exactly what made resume unreachable; docs updated in the same change.\\n- **The gateway client surfaces non-101 upgrade responses** ([gateway-client/client.ts](../blob/HEAD/packages/gateway-client/src/client.ts)): bounded body read (2 KiB / 1 s), typed retryable `GatewayClientRequestError` (`websocket-upgrade-rejected`, HTTP status) carried through close/retry state so CLI callers see the real reason during restart drain.\\n- **New CLI commands** ([suspend-cli.ts](../blob/HEAD/src/cli/gateway-cli/suspend-cli.ts)): `openclaw gateway suspend` (stable request id, `--wait <seconds>` bounded polling driven by server `retryAfterMs`, blocker listing, `--json`) and `openclaw gateway resume <suspensionId>` (idempotent; expired lease reported as a clean no-op).\\n\\nProduction LOC delta is positive (~+285 excluding tests/docs/generated) and buys the new CLI capability plus the client error surface; the core gate change itself is net ~+14. The additive `connectError` field on `GatewayClientCloseInfo` regenerated the plugin-sdk API baseline.\\n\\n## User Impact\\n\\nOperators and hosting controllers can suspend and resume the Gateway end to end from the CLI: prepare before a VM snapshot/process freeze, resume from a *fresh* process after thaw. Failures name themselves: a suspended Gateway now answers \\\"`health` unavailable during gateway suspension (reason gateway-suspending, phase prepared, retryable)\\\" instead of an unexplained 1006 close. Lost-id worst case remains the 2-minute self-healing lease expiry.\\n\\n## Evidence\\n\\nLive end-to-end on an isolated dev gateway (dedicated `OPENCLAW_STATE_DIR`, port 19877, dist build):\\n\\n- `openclaw gateway suspend` → ready with suspension id; real `SIGSTOP`/`SIGCONT` freeze; `openclaw gateway resume <id>` from a fresh process → \\\"Gateway resumed.\\\"; health green over both WS and admin HTTP.\\n- While prepared: fresh-WS `gateway call health` refused with structured retryable `UNAVAILABLE` (reason `gateway-suspending`, phase `prepared`).\\n- 150 s freeze past the 120 s TTL → lease self-expires on thaw, admission reopens, late resume returns `resumed:false`, health green.\\n- Conflict (second prepare, different request id), idempotent renew (same request id), wrong-id resume (`INVALID_REQUEST`), no-suspension resume (clean no-op) all verified live.\\n\\nTests/gates (run locally; the delegated `check:changed` Testbox lane fails on its own merge-base resolution before running lanes — env-var ratchet proven locally, 502/502):\\n\\n- Focused vitest: server-methods.suspension-admission 6/6, plugins-http.suspension-admission 8/8, gateway-suspend-coordinator 13/13, message-handler.suspension-admission 5/5, server.preauth-hardening 10/10 (prepared-accept, preparing-reject, restart-reject, worker-reject), client.handshake 4/4, suspend-cli 7/7, register.option-collisions 17/17, gateway/call 133/133.\\n- `pnpm tsgo:core`, `tsgo:core:test`, oxlint on changed files, `format:check`, `check:import-cycles`, `plugin-sdk:api:check` + `surface:check`, and the remaining changed-lane aux checks all green.\\n- `pnpm build` clean (no `[INEFFECTIVE_DYNAMIC_IMPORT]`).\\n- Codex autoreview (Sol, high): clean, no actionable findings.\\n\\nFollow-up commits on this branch: the `connectError` addition pushed `protocol-client.ts` over the 700-line max-lines gate, so the adapter-facing contract types moved to a new `protocol-client-contract.ts` (no suppression; 220/220 gateway-client tests green, SDK baselines regenerated). This PR also repairs red `main`: `src/agents/mcp-oauth-refresh-issuer.test.ts` fails `check-docs` oxfmt on current main (drift from #112032's file); fixed here per red-main landing policy.\\n\",
